## Researcher–agent collaboration



Chronological shared conversation. The researcher sees these handoffs, not private tool output. Full tool and model records follow.

### 1. Researcher — initial_request

Please inspect the staged repository and relevant data files, especially capital.csv and any analysis code or documentation for the PCA in 100-year bins. Report the available columns, data shape/coverage and missingness relevant to house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords; identify how the PCA was conducted or what would be needed to reproduce it; and propose a concise analysis approach to determine these variables’ PC1 values and their distribution on PC2. Please also flag consequential assumptions or uncertainties. Do not yet perform the full analysis or report a substantive finding.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** initial_inspection; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The brief specifies the target variables and 100-year PCA slices, but supplies dataset descriptions rather than PCA results or implementation details. Initial inspection should establish the staged data and reproducible analysis options before authorizing the main work.

**Open objectives:** Inspect repository and staged data relevant to capital PCA; Establish PCA implementation details or identify consequential ambiguities; Propose analysis approach for PC1 values and PC2 distribution

**Agent reply (plan)**

Repository inspection found only the three staged CSVs and task metadata—no PCA scripts, notebooks, or documentation. `capital.csv` has 39 rows and 15 columns: `BCE`, `group`, `color`, and 12 capital-indicator columns. Its time points are every 100 years from 4300 to 500 BCE (39 unique values); `ZAxtSchwert` (hatchets/swords), `ZDolch` (daggers), and `Zhausgr` (house size) are present for all 39 rows with no reported blanks. Their fields currently parse as strings because decimal commas are used. `group` and `color` appear to be categorical period/plot metadata and are described in the supplied metadata as dropped after loading.

Proposed workflow after approval: parse the decimal-comma indicator values to numeric; treat each BCE row as a hundred-year slice; use the 12 `Z...` capital indicators as the variables and slices as observations; reproduce PCA on the standardized or already-z-scored columns using the most defensible available procedure; report the focal indicators’ loadings on PC1 and PC2, with signs and magnitude, plus whether their PC2 coordinates share a direction or differ. Also inspect variance explained and check robustness to data preprocessing / row direction. I will not compute the PCA or report a substantive finding in this inspection stage.

Important uncertainty: the requested phrase “values on PC1 and distribution across PC2” could refer to variable loadings (PCA variable coordinates) or the 39 time-slice scores. There is no code or method note to resolve this. The columns are labeled Z values, but the data alone does not establish whether they should be re-standardized before PCA, nor whether PCA was fit jointly across all slices or separately within each 100-year slice. Because there is one row per slice, independent within-slice PCA would not be meaningful; the proposed default is one PCA across the 39 slices with indicators as features, and to distinguish feature loadings from time-slice scores explicitly.

**Shared evidence:**

Inspected /workspace file inventory; only task.json and three CSVs present. pandas read_csv('data/capital.csv') yielded shape (39,15), BCE min/max 500/4300 with 39 unique values; target fields showed 0 missing each. All capital indicator fields were object/string and head values use commas as decimal marks, e.g. '-0,44'. No separate analysis code or documentation was found outside task.json.

### 2. Researcher — next_task

Thanks; the inspection supports a clear default: perform a single PCA across the 39 hundred-year slices, with the 12 capital indicators as features. Please now parse decimal-comma values and conduct the PCA. Since these columns are already labeled Z values but scaling intent is undocumented, report the primary result using the defensible stated preprocessing (and explicitly say whether/how variables were standardized), and a brief sensitivity check if feasible. Focus on the three symbolic indicators’ PC1 coordinates and their PC2 coordinates/distribution, distinguishing feature loadings/coordinates from time-slice scores. Include explained variance and any relevant sign-orientation caveat; do not infer undocumented study intent.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** True

**Basis:** The reported inventory shows 39 complete time slices and the 12 capital variables, but no PCA implementation or method documentation. The plan correctly identifies ambiguity between variable coordinates and sample scores and uncertainty about scaling; a joint PCA across slices is feasible and avoids meaningless per-slice PCA.

**Open objectives:** Compute and report PCA variable coordinates/loadings for HouseSize, Dagger, and HatchetSword on PC1 and PC2, with preprocessing and sign convention.; Characterize whether the three symbolic indicators are similarly or differently distributed along PC2; provide explained variance and clarify distinction from time-slice scores.; Address scaling uncertainty with transparent preprocessing and a concise sensitivity check if feasible.

**Agent reply (result)**

I parsed decimal-comma values in `capital.csv` and ran a single PCA across the 39 time slices (4300–500 BCE, in 100-year increments), using the 12 capital indicators as features. No rows or features were missing after conversion.

**Primary result (each feature centered and scaled to unit variance):** PC1 explains 30.33% and PC2 19.47% of variance (49.79% cumulative). Feature coordinates are eigenvector × √eigenvalue (equivalent here to feature–component correlations because input features have unit variance):

| Indicator | PC1 coordinate | PC2 coordinate |
|---|---:|---:|
| House size (`Zhausgr`) | +0.429 | +0.500 |
| Daggers (`ZDolch`) | +0.507 | +0.113 |
| Hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) | +0.570 | +0.082 |

Thus all three point positively on PC1 in the stated orientation; hatchets/swords and daggers have stronger PC1 coordinates than house size. On PC2, house size has a notably larger positive coordinate, while daggers and hatchets/swords are only weakly positive—so their separation on PC2 is mainly house size versus the two weapon variables, rather than opposite directions among the three. These are **feature coordinates/loadings**, not scores for the 39 time slices.

For distinction, the **time-slice PC2 scores** are spread on both sides of zero: 16 negative and 23 positive, median +0.340 and range −3.648 to +2.503. In broad BCE ranges, average PC2 scores are −0.309 (500–1400), −0.262 (1500–2400), +1.198 (2500–3400), and −0.697 (3500–4300); that last range includes the most negative three early slices (4100–4300 BCE), each at −3.648. This summarizes slice scores, not the feature-coordinate distribution.

**Sensitivity:** Since the recorded fields are already Z values and 11 of 12 have sample SD near 1, I also centered but did not rescale them. This gives PC1/PC2 variance explained of 31.84%/20.42% (52.26% cumulative); focal feature coordinates remain positive and very similar: house size +0.432/+0.552, daggers +0.529/+0.181, hatchets/swords +0.570/+0.101. The main scale exception is sickles (`ZSichel`, SD 0.538); therefore unit-variance scaling is a defensible primary choice for equal indicator weighting, while centered-only is a useful robustness check. The focal conclusion is insensitive to that choice.

**Caveats:** PCA signs are arbitrary; signs above use a reproducible display convention (for each axis, make the largest-absolute feature coordinate positive). Reversing an axis reverses every sign on that axis without changing relationships. The repository contains no original PCA code or explanation of whether the published analysis scaled its Z variables; these are a transparent reconstruction, not a claim about undocumented intent. I report eigenvector-scaled feature coordinates; an unscaled eigenvector table would have different magnitudes, although its within-axis relative signs/order agree. Time-slice scores likewise use the stated orientation.

**Shared evidence:**

Executed analysis.py successfully in the workspace. Parsed numeric matrix shape was 39x12 with zero missing values. Unit-variance explained variance first two components was [0.30327843, 0.19465600], cumulative 0.49793443; focal feature coordinates and time-slice PC2 score summary are included above and printed by the script. Center-only scaling sensitivity results are also reported.

### 3. Researcher — conclude

The reported analysis addresses the question: across 39 hundred-year slices and 12 capital indicators, house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords all have positive PC1 feature coordinates in the stated orientation, with the two weapon indicators stronger than house size. On PC2, house size is more strongly positive, while the two weapon indicators are only weakly positive. The response distinguishes feature coordinates from time-slice scores and reports explained variance, preprocessing, a scaling sensitivity check, and the arbitrary-sign caveat. This is an adequate data-grounded hypothesis; no revision is needed.

**Decision:** stop

**Review phase:** result_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The agent reports decimal-comma parsing with no missing values, PCA across the stated observations/features, focal PC1/PC2 feature coordinates and variance explained, a centered-only sensitivity analysis with similar focal results, and clear distinction from PC2 time-slice scores. No identifiable mismatch with the agreed approach is apparent.

**Open objectives:** None

for standardized total vars)\\nXs=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\npca=PCA().fit(Xs)\\nscore=pca.transform(Xs)\\nlam=pca.explained_variance_\\ncoord=pca.components_.T*np.sqrt(lam)\\n# Corr coordinates for standardized feature values in PC scores\\ncorr=np.corrcoef(Xs.T,score.T)[:len(features),len(features):]\\nprint('STANDARDIZED EVR first five',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:5], 'cumulative',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum())\\nprint('STANDARDIZED target coordinates: eigenvector-scaled; correlations\\\\n',pd.DataFrame({'PC1_coord':coord[:,0],'PC2_coord':coord[:,1],'PC1_corr':corr[:,0],'PC2_corr':corr[:,1]},index=features).loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_string())\\nprint('PC1 PC2 variance',lam[:2])\\nprint('scores summary and extremes')\\nss=pd.DataFrame(score[:,:2],columns=['PC1_score','PC2_score']); ss['BCE']=df.BCE\\nprint(ss[['PC1_score','PC2_score']].describe().to_string())\\nprint('PC2 sorted target epochs')\\nprint(ss.sort_values('PC2_score')[['BCE','PC1_score','PC2_score']].to_string(index=False))\\n# ensure orientation chosen such that PC1 hatchetSword positive? report conventional from sklearn deterministic, orientation arbitrary\\n# sensitivity: PCA on z inputs without scaling (center only)\\npca2=PCA().fit(X)\\nrawcoord=pca2.components_.T*np.sqrt(pca2.explained_variance_)\\nprint('\\\\nCENTERED-ONLY EVR first two',pca2.explained_variance_ratio_[:2], 'cum',pca2.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum())\\nprint('CENTERED-ONLY target coords\\\\n',pd.DataFrame({'PC1_coord':rawcoord[:,0],'PC2_coord':rawcoord[:,1]},index=features).loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_string())\\nprint('input SD target',X.std().loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_dict())\\n# Report PCA score PC2 distribution bins by BCE centuries & sign fractions\\nprint('PC2 signs standardized',dict(zip(*np.unique(np.sign(score[:,1]),return_counts=True))))\\n# correlations standardized indicators with component scores as interpretable coordinates\\nPY\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}",

{
  "id": "call_MvIgNmrZGOJWTRHXwHqcnWE5",
  "responses_item_id": null,
  "name": "execute_command",
  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Run covariance PCA using NumPy eigen decomposition and summarize coordinates\", \"command\": \"cat > /tmp/calc.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd, numpy as np\\nXdf=pd.read_csv('data/capital.csv')\\nfeatures=Xdf.columns[3:].tolist()\\nX=Xdf[features].apply(lambda s:pd.to_numeric(s.astype(str).str.replace(',','.',regex=False)))\\nA=X.to_numpy(float)\\nprint('numeric matrix',A.shape,'missing',np.isnan(A).sum(),'features',features)\\ndef pca(A,standardize):\\n  m=A.mean(axis=0); sd=A.std(axis=0,ddof=1)\\n  B=(A-m)/sd if standardize else (A-m)\\n  cov=B.T@B/(len(A)-1)\\n  ev,V=np.linalg.eigh(cov); ix=np.argsort(ev)[::-1]; ev=ev[ix]; V=V[:,ix]\\n  # Orient each axis by largest absolute loading positive (deterministic convention)\\n  for j in range(V.shape[1]):\\n    k=np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,j]))\\n    if V[k,j]<0: V[:,j]*=-1\\n  scores=B@V\\n  coords=V*np.sqrt(ev)[None,:]\\n  cors=np.corrcoef(B.T,scores.T)[:len(features),len(features):]\\n  return ev,V,scores,coords,cors\\nfor std in (True,False):\\n ev,V,S,C,R=pca(A,std)\\n tag='UNIT_VARIANCE' if std else 'CENTER_ONLY'\\n print('\\\\n',tag,'EVR',np.round(ev/ev.sum(),6),'first2',np.round(ev[:2]/ev.sum(),6),'cum2',round((ev[:2]/ev.sum()).sum(),6))\\n for f in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n  i=features.index(f); print(f,'coords',np.round(C[i,:2],6),'correlations',np.round(R[i,:2],6),'eigenvector',np.round(V[i,:2],6))\\n if std:\\n  print('score quantiles',pd.DataFrame(S[:,:2],columns=['PC1','PC2']).quantile([0,.25,.5,.75,1]).round(4).to_string())\\n  # PC2 time periods distribution: signed counts, listed epochs at extrema and coarse bins\\n  print('PC2 score by BCE extremes')\\n  z=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':Xdf.BCE,'PC1score':S[:,0],'PC2score':S[:,1]})\\n  print(z.sort_values('PC2score')[['BCE','PC2score']].to_string(index=False))\\n  print('PC2 sign',{'negative':int((S[:,1]<0).sum()),'positive':int((S[:,1]>0).sum()),'zero':int((S[:,1]==0).sum())})\\nprint('\\\\nSD raw',X.std().round(4).to_dict())\\nPY\\npython /tmp/calc.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}",

{
  "id": "call_HQvhoRwqcdAabxY1vHhXayyt",
  "responses_item_id": null,
  "name": "execute_command",
  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Save executed PCA script and add interpretable component coordinates\", \"command\": \"cat > analysis.py <<'PY'\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"PCA of archaeological capital indicators across 100-year slices.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\n\\nDATA = 'data/capital.csv'\\ndf = pd.read_csv(DATA)\\nfeatures = df.columns[3:].tolist()\\n# CSV uses decimal commas; preserve BCE, group, color as metadata.\\nXdf = df[features].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.astype(str).str.replace(',', '.', regex=False)))\\nX = Xdf.to_numpy(dtype=float)\\nassert X.shape == (39, 12) and np.isfinite(X).all()\\n\\ndef pca(matrix, scale):\\n    mean = matrix.mean(axis=0)\\n    sd = matrix.std(axis=0, ddof=1)\\n    z = (matrix - mean) / sd if scale else matrix - mean\\n    cov = z.T @ z / (len(z) - 1)\\n    vals, vecs = np.linalg.eigh(cov)\\n    order = np.argsort(vals)[::-1]\\n    vals, vecs = vals[order], vecs[:, order]\\n    # Explicit deterministic display orientation: largest absolute loading positive.\\n    for j in range(vecs.shape[1]):\\n        k = np.argmax(np.abs(vecs[:, j]))\\n        if vecs[k, j] < 0:\\n            vecs[:, j] *= -1\\n    scores = z @ vecs\\n    feature_coordinates = vecs * np.sqrt(vals)[None, :]\\n    return vals, vecs, scores, feature_coordinates\\n\\nfor standardize, label in [(True, 'unit-variance'), (False, 'center-only')]:\\n    eigenvalues, vectors, scores, coords = pca(X, standardize)\\n    print(f'\\\\n{label}: PC1/PC2 variance explained = '\\n          f'{eigenvalues[:2] / eigenvalues.sum()} (cumulative '\\n          f'{eigenvalues[:2].sum() / eigenvalues.sum():.6f})')\\n    print('Feature coordinates (eigenvector * sqrt(eigenvalue)); rows are indicators:')\\n    for f in ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']:\\n        i = features.index(f)\\n        print(f, 'PC1', coords[i, 0], 'PC2', coords[i, 1])\\n    if standardize:\\n        score_table = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df.BCE, 'PC1_score': scores[:, 0], 'PC2_score': scores[:, 1]})\\n        print('Time-slice score ranges:')\\n        print(score_table[['PC1_score', 'PC2_score']].agg(['min', 'median', 'max']).to_string())\\n        print('PC2 sign counts:', int((scores[:, 1] < 0).sum()), 'negative,',\\n              int((scores[:, 1] > 0).sum()), 'positive')\\n        for low, high in [(500, 1400), (1500, 2400), (2500, 3400), (3500, 4300)]:\\n            sel = score_table.BCE.between(low, high).to_numpy()\\n            vals = scores[sel, 1]\\n            print(f'BCE {low}-{high}: n={len(vals)}, PC2-score mean={vals.mean():.4f}, '\\n                  f'range=({vals.min():.4f},{vals.max():.4f})')\\n        print('Largest-magnitude feature coordinates PC1/PC2:')\\n        for j in range(2):\\n            ix = np.argsort(np.abs(coords[:, j]))[::-1][:5]\\n            print('PC', j + 1, [(features[i], round(coords[i, j], 4)) for i in ix])\\nprint('\\\\nRaw indicator sample SDs:')\\nprint(Xdf.std().round(4).to_string())\\nPY\\npython analysis.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}",

}

{
  "command": "cat > analysis.py <<'PY'\n\"\"\"PCA of archaeological capital indicators across 100-year slices.\"\"\"\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\nDATA = 'data/capital.csv'\ndf = pd.read_csv(DATA)\nfeatures = df.columns[3:].tolist()\n# CSV uses decimal commas; preserve BCE, group, color as metadata.\nXdf = df[features].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.astype(str).str.replace(',', '.', regex=False)))\nX = Xdf.to_numpy(dtype=float)\nassert X.shape == (39, 12) and np.isfinite(X).all()\n\ndef pca(matrix, scale):\n    mean = matrix.mean(axis=0)\n    sd = matrix.std(axis=0, ddof=1)\n    z = (matrix - mean) / sd if scale else matrix - mean\n    cov = z.T @ z / (len(z) - 1)\n    vals, vecs = np.linalg.eigh(cov)\n    order = np.argsort(vals)[::-1]\n    vals, vecs = vals[order], vecs[:, order]\n    # Explicit deterministic display orientation: largest absolute loading positive.\n    for j in range(vecs.shape[1]):\n        k = np.argmax(np.abs(vecs[:, j]))\n        if vecs[k, j] < 0:\n            vecs[:, j] *= -1\n    scores = z @ vecs\n    feature_coordinates = vecs * np.sqrt(vals)[None, :]\n    return vals, vecs, scores, feature_coordinates\n\nfor standardize, label in [(True, 'unit-variance'), (False, 'center-only')]:\n    eigenvalues, vectors, scores, coords = pca(X, standardize)\n    print(f'\\n{label}: PC1/PC2 variance explained = '\n          f'{eigenvalues[:2] / eigenvalues.sum()} (cumulative '\n          f'{eigenvalues[:2].sum() / eigenvalues.sum():.6f})')\n    print('Feature coordinates (eigenvector * sqrt(eigenvalue)); rows are indicators:')\n    for f in ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']:\n        i = features.index(f)\n        print(f, 'PC1', coords[i, 0], 'PC2', coords[i, 1])\n    if standardize:\n        score_table = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df.BCE, 'PC1_score': scores[:, 0], 'PC2_score': scores[:, 1]})\n        print('Time-slice score ranges:')\n        print(score_table[['PC1_score', 'PC2_score']].agg(['min', 'median', 'max']).to_string())\n        print('PC2 sign counts:', int((scores[:, 1] < 0).sum()), 'negative,',\n              int((scores[:, 1] > 0).sum()), 'positive')\n        for low, high in [(500, 1400), (1500, 2400), (2500, 3400), (3500, 4300)]:\n            sel = score_table.BCE.between(low, high).to_numpy()\n            vals = scores[sel, 1]\n            print(f'BCE {low}-{high}: n={len(vals)}, PC2-score mean={vals.mean():.4f}, '\n                  f'range=({vals.min():.4f},{vals.max():.4f})')\n        print('Largest-magnitude feature coordinates PC1/PC2:')\n        for j in range(2):\n            ix = np.argsort(np.abs(coords[:, j]))[::-1][:5]\n            print('PC', j + 1, [(features[i], round(coords[i, j], 4)) for i in ix])\nprint('\\nRaw indicator sample SDs:')\nprint(Xdf.std().round(4).to_string())\nPY\npython analysis.py",

}

{
  "answer": "I parsed decimal-comma values in `capital.csv` and ran a single PCA across the 39 time slices (4300–500 BCE, in 100-year increments), using the 12 capital indicators as features. No rows or features were missing after conversion.\n\n**Primary result (each feature centered and scaled to unit variance):** PC1 explains 30.33% and PC2 19.47% of variance (49.79% cumulative). Feature coordinates are eigenvector × √eigenvalue (equivalent here to feature–component correlations because input features have unit variance):\n\n| Indicator | PC1 coordinate | PC2 coordinate |\n|---|---:|---:|\n| House size (`Zhausgr`) | +0.429 | +0.500 |\n| Daggers (`ZDolch`) | +0.507 | +0.113 |\n| Hatchets/swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) | +0.570 | +0.082 |\n\nThus all three point positively on PC1 in the stated orientation; hatchets/swords and daggers have stronger PC1 coordinates than house size. On PC2, house size has a notably larger positive coordinate, while daggers and hatchets/swords are only weakly positive—so their separation on PC2 is mainly house size versus the two weapon variables, rather than opposite directions among the three. These are **feature coordinates/loadings**, not scores for the 39 time slices.\n\nFor distinction, the **time-slice PC2 scores** are spread on both sides of zero: 16 negative and 23 positive, median +0.340 and range −3.648 to +2.503. In broad BCE ranges, average PC2 scores are −0.309 (500–1400), −0.262 (1500–2400), +1.198 (2500–3400), and −0.697 (3500–4300); that last range includes the most negative three early slices (4100–4300 BCE), each at −3.648. This summarizes slice scores, not the feature-coordinate distribution.\n\n**Sensitivity:** Since the recorded fields are already Z values and 11 of 12 have sample SD near 1, I also centered but did not rescale them. This gives PC1/PC2 variance explained of 31.84%/20.42% (52.26% cumulative); focal feature coordinates remain positive and very similar: house size +0.432/+0.552, daggers +0.529/+0.181, hatchets/swords +0.570/+0.101. The main scale exception is sickles (`ZSichel`, SD 0.538); therefore unit-variance scaling is a defensible primary choice for equal indicator weighting, while centered-only is a useful robustness check. The focal conclusion is insensitive to that choice.\n\n**Caveats:** PCA signs are arbitrary; signs above use a reproducible display convention (for each axis, make the largest-absolute feature coordinate positive). Reversing an axis reverses every sign on that axis without changing relationships. The repository contains no original PCA code or explanation of whether the published analysis scaled its Z variables; these are a transparent reconstruction, not a claim about undocumented intent. I report eigenvector-scaled feature coordinates; an unscaled eigenvector table would have different magnitudes, although its within-axis relative signs/order agree. Time-slice scores likewise use the stated orientation.",

<details>
<summary>Request message 1: System</summary>

````text
You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. for standardized total vars)\\nXs=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\npca=PCA().fit(Xs)\\nscore=pca.transform(Xs)\\nlam=pca.explained_variance_\\ncoord=pca.components_.T*np.sqrt(lam)\\n# Corr coordinates for standardized feature values in PC scores\\ncorr=np.corrcoef(Xs.T,score.T)[:len(features),len(features):]\\nprint('STANDARDIZED EVR first five',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:5], 'cumulative',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum())\\nprint('STANDARDIZED target coordinates: eigenvector-scaled; correlations\\\\n',pd.DataFrame({'PC1_coord':coord[:,0],'PC2_coord':coord[:,1],'PC1_corr':corr[:,0],'PC2_corr':corr[:,1]},index=features).loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_string())\\nprint('PC1 PC2 variance',lam[:2])\\nprint('scores summary and extremes')\\nss=pd.DataFrame(score[:,:2],columns=['PC1_score','PC2_score']); ss['BCE']=df.BCE\\nprint(ss[['PC1_score','PC2_score']].describe().to_string())\\nprint('PC2 sorted target epochs')\\nprint(ss.sort_values('PC2_score')[['BCE','PC1_score','PC2_score']].to_string(index=False))\\n# ensure orientation chosen such that PC1 hatchetSword positive? for standardized total vars)\\nXs=StandardScaler().fit_transform(X)\\npca=PCA().fit(Xs)\\nscore=pca.transform(Xs)\\nlam=pca.explained_variance_\\ncoord=pca.components_.T*np.sqrt(lam)\\n# Corr coordinates for standardized feature values in PC scores\\ncorr=np.corrcoef(Xs.T,score.T)[:len(features),len(features):]\\nprint('STANDARDIZED EVR first five',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:5], 'cumulative',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum())\\nprint('STANDARDIZED target coordinates: eigenvector-scaled; correlations\\\\n',pd.DataFrame({'PC1_coord':coord[:,0],'PC2_coord':coord[:,1],'PC1_corr':corr[:,0],'PC2_corr':corr[:,1]},index=features).loc[['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']].to_string())\\nprint('PC1 PC2 variance',lam[:2])\\nprint('scores summary and extremes')\\nss=pd.DataFrame(score[:,:2],columns=['PC1_score','PC2_score']); ss['BCE']=df.BCE\\nprint(ss[['PC1_score','PC2_score']].describe().to_string())\\nprint('PC2 sorted target epochs')\\nprint(ss.sort_values('PC2_score')[['BCE','PC1_score','PC2_score']].to_string(index=False))\\n# ensure orientation chosen such that PC1 hatchetSword positive? sd=A.std(axis=0,ddof=1)\\n  B=(A-m)/sd if standardize else (A-m)\\n  cov=B.T@B/(len(A)-1)\\n  ev,V=np.linalg.eigh(cov); ix=np.argsort(ev)[::-1]; ev=ev[ix]; V=V[:,ix]\\n  # Orient each axis by largest absolute loading positive (deterministic convention)\\n  for j in range(V.shape[1]):\\n    k=np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,j]))\\n    if V[k,j]<0: V[:,j]*=-1\\n  scores=B@V\\n  coords=V*np.sqrt(ev)[None,:]\\n  cors=np.corrcoef(B.T,scores.T)[:len(features),len(features):]\\n  return ev,V,scores,coords,cors\\nfor std in (True,False):\\n ev,V,S,C,R=pca(A,std)\\n tag='UNIT_VARIANCE' if std else 'CENTER_ONLY'\\n print('\\\\n',tag,'EVR',np.round(ev/ev.sum(),6),'first2',np.round(ev[:2]/ev.sum(),6),'cum2',round((ev[:2]/ev.sum()).sum(),6))\\n for f in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n  i=features.index(f); sd=A.std(axis=0,ddof=1)\\n  B=(A-m)/sd if standardize else (A-m)\\n  cov=B.T@B/(len(A)-1)\\n  ev,V=np.linalg.eigh(cov); ix=np.argsort(ev)[::-1]; ev=ev[ix]; V=V[:,ix]\\n  # Orient each axis by largest absolute loading positive (deterministic convention)\\n  for j in range(V.shape[1]):\\n    k=np.argmax(np.abs(V[:,j]))\\n    if V[k,j]<0: V[:,j]*=-1\\n  scores=B@V\\n  coords=V*np.sqrt(ev)[None,:]\\n  cors=np.corrcoef(B.T,scores.T)[:len(features),len(features):]\\n  return ev,V,scores,coords,cors\\nfor std in (True,False):\\n ev,V,S,C,R=pca(A,std)\\n tag='UNIT_VARIANCE' if std else 'CENTER_ONLY'\\n print('\\\\n',tag,'EVR',np.round(ev/ev.sum(),6),'first2',np.round(ev[:2]/ev.sum(),6),'cum2',round((ev[:2]/ev.sum()).sum(),6))\\n for f in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n  i=features.index(f); print(f,'coords',np.round(C[i,:2],6),'correlations',np.round(R[i,:2],6),'eigenvector',np.round(V[i,:2],6))\\n if std:\\n  print('score quantiles',pd.DataFrame(S[:,:2],columns=['PC1','PC2']).quantile([0,.25,.5,.75,1]).round(4).to_string())\\n  # PC2 time periods distribution: signed counts, listed epochs at extrema and coarse bins\\n  print('PC2 score by BCE extremes')\\n  z=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':Xdf.BCE,'PC1score':S[:,0],'PC2score':S[:,1]})\\n  print(z.sort_values('PC2score')[['BCE','PC2score']].to_string(index=False))\\n  print('PC2 sign',{'negative':int((S[:,1]<0).sum()),'positive':int((S[:,1]>0).sum()),'zero':int((S[:,1]==0).sum())})\\nprint('\\\\nSD raw',X.std().round(4).to_dict())\\nPY\\npython /tmp/calc.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

{

      "function": {
        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Save executed PCA script and add interpretable component coordinates\",\"command\":\"cat > analysis.py <<'PY'\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"PCA of archaeological capital indicators across 100-year slices.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\n\\nDATA = 'data/capital.csv'\\ndf = pd.read_csv(DATA)\\nfeatures = df.columns[3:].tolist()\\n# CSV uses decimal commas; preserve BCE, group, color as metadata.\\nXdf = df[features].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.astype(str).str.replace(',', '.', regex=False)))\\nX = Xdf.to_numpy(dtype=float)\\nassert X.shape == (39, 12) and np.isfinite(X).all()\\n\\ndef pca(matrix, scale):\\n    mean = matrix.mean(axis=0)\\n    sd = matrix.std(axis=0, ddof=1)\\n    z = (matrix - mean) / sd if scale else matrix - mean\\n    cov = z.T @ z / (len(z) - 1)\\n    vals, vecs = np.linalg.eigh(cov)\\n    order = np.argsort(vals)[::-1]\\n    vals, vecs = vals[order], vecs[:, order]\\n    # Explicit deterministic display orientation: largest absolute loading positive.\\n    for j in range(vecs.shape[1]):\\n        k = np.argmax(np.abs(vecs[:, j]))\\n        if vecs[k, j] < 0:\\n            vecs[:, j] *= -1\\n    scores = z @ vecs\\n    feature_coordinates = vecs * np.sqrt(vals)[None, :]\\n    return vals, vecs, scores, feature_coordinates\\n\\nfor standardize, label in [(True, 'unit-variance'), (False, 'center-only')]:\\n    eigenvalues, vectors, scores, coords = pca(X, standardize)\\n    print(f'\\\\n{label}: PC1/PC2 variance explained = '\\n          f'{eigenvalues[:2] / eigenvalues.sum()} (cumulative '\\n          f'{eigenvalues[:2].sum() / eigenvalues.sum():.6f})')\\n    print('Feature coordinates (eigenvector * sqrt(eigenvalue)); rows are indicators:')\\n    for f in ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']:\\n        i = features.index(f)\\n        print(f, 'PC1', coords[i, 0], 'PC2', coords[i, 1])\\n    if standardize:\\n        score_table = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df.BCE, 'PC1_score': scores[:, 0], 'PC2_score': scores[:, 1]})\\n        print('Time-slice score ranges:')\\n        print(score_table[['PC1_score', 'PC2_score']].agg(['min', 'median', 'max']).to_string())\\n        print('PC2 sign counts:', int((scores[:, 1] < 0).sum()), 'negative,',\\n              int((scores[:, 1] > 0).sum()), 'positive')\\n        for low, high in [(500, 1400), (1500, 2400), (2500, 3400), (3500, 4300)]:\\n            sel = score_table.BCE.between(low, high).to_numpy()\\n            vals = scores[sel, 1]\\n            print(f'BCE {low}-{high}: n={len(vals)}, PC2-score mean={vals.mean():.4f}, '\\n                  f'range=({vals.min():.4f},{vals.max():.4f})')\\n        print('Largest-magnitude feature coordinates PC1/PC2:')\\n        for j in range(2):\\n            ix = np.argsort(np.abs(coords[:, j]))[::-1][:5]\\n            print('PC', j + 1, [(features[i], round(coords[i, j], 4)) for i in ix])\\nprint('\\\\nRaw indicator sample SDs:')\\nprint(Xdf.std().round(4).to_string())\\nPY\\npython analysis.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":120}"
      }
    }
  ]
}

        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Save executed PCA script and add interpretable component coordinates\", \"command\": \"cat > analysis.py <<'PY'\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"PCA of archaeological capital indicators across 100-year slices.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\n\\nDATA = 'data/capital.csv'\\ndf = pd.read_csv(DATA)\\nfeatures = df.columns[3:].tolist()\\n# CSV uses decimal commas; preserve BCE, group, color as metadata.\\nXdf = df[features].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.astype(str).str.replace(',', '.', regex=False)))\\nX = Xdf.to_numpy(dtype=float)\\nassert X.shape == (39, 12) and np.isfinite(X).all()\\n\\ndef pca(matrix, scale):\\n    mean = matrix.mean(axis=0)\\n    sd = matrix.std(axis=0, ddof=1)\\n    z = (matrix - mean) / sd if scale else matrix - mean\\n    cov = z.T @ z / (len(z) - 1)\\n    vals, vecs = np.linalg.eigh(cov)\\n    order = np.argsort(vals)[::-1]\\n    vals, vecs = vals[order], vecs[:, order]\\n    # Explicit deterministic display orientation: largest absolute loading positive.\\n    for j in range(vecs.shape[1]):\\n        k = np.argmax(np.abs(vecs[:, j]))\\n        if vecs[k, j] < 0:\\n            vecs[:, j] *= -1\\n    scores = z @ vecs\\n    feature_coordinates = vecs * np.sqrt(vals)[None, :]\\n    return vals, vecs, scores, feature_coordinates\\n\\nfor standardize, label in [(True, 'unit-variance'), (False, 'center-only')]:\\n    eigenvalues, vectors, scores, coords = pca(X, standardize)\\n    print(f'\\\\n{label}: PC1/PC2 variance explained = '\\n          f'{eigenvalues[:2] / eigenvalues.sum()} (cumulative '\\n          f'{eigenvalues[:2].sum() / eigenvalues.sum():.6f})')\\n    print('Feature coordinates (eigenvector * sqrt(eigenvalue)); rows are indicators:')\\n    for f in ['Zhausgr', 'ZDolch', 'ZAxtSchwert']:\\n        i = features.index(f)\\n        print(f, 'PC1', coords[i, 0], 'PC2', coords[i, 1])\\n    if standardize:\\n        score_table = pd.DataFrame({'BCE': df.BCE, 'PC1_score': scores[:, 0], 'PC2_score': scores[:, 1]})\\n        print('Time-slice score ranges:')\\n        print(score_table[['PC1_score', 'PC2_score']].agg(['min', 'median', 'max']).to_string())\\n        print('PC2 sign counts:', int((scores[:, 1] < 0).sum()), 'negative,',\\n              int((scores[:, 1] > 0).sum()), 'positive')\\n        for low, high in [(500, 1400), (1500, 2400), (2500, 3400), (3500, 4300)]:\\n            sel = score_table.BCE.between(low, high).to_numpy()\\n            vals = scores[sel, 1]\\n            print(f'BCE {low}-{high}: n={len(vals)}, PC2-score mean={vals.mean():.4f}, '\\n                  f'range=({vals.min():.4f},{vals.max():.4f})')\\n        print('Largest-magnitude feature coordinates PC1/PC2:')\\n        for j in range(2):\\n            ix = np.argsort(np.abs(coords[:, j]))[::-1][:5]\\n            print('PC', j + 1, [(features[i], round(coords[i, j], 4)) for i in ix])\\nprint('\\\\nRaw indicator sample SDs:')\\nprint(Xdf.std().round(4).to_string())\\nPY\\npython analysis.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 120}"

Compare HypoA and HypoB in terms of three aspects: Contexts, Variables, and Relations.         E.g., for the hypothesis "From 1995 to 2009, the number of sandhill cranes around the tundra (Indigilka River) surged by an astounding ~10X":
        * Contexts refer to stratification of the data under which the given hypothesis is True. E.g., "For all women", "From 1995 to 2009".
        * Variables refer to the set of variables (either dependent or independent) that are mentioned in the hypothesis. E.g., number of sandhill cranes, location.
        * Relations refer to the form of relation between the variables. E.g., "surged by ~10x".
